[PATCH 0/7] locking: opt-in tracking of sleeping lock holders Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch series introduces a mechanism to track sleeping lock holders in the Linux kernel, allowing tasks to determine if they hold a lock that other tasks may be waiting for. This is done by adding a count to task_struct for locks that opt-in to being tracked. The goal is to improve shared lock isolation and prevent pressure from one workload leaking into unrelated workloads through shared locks. The patch addresses the problem of stalls caused by widely shared locks, which can slow down not just the holding task but also other tasks waiting behind it. By knowing if a task holds such a lock, the allocator can treat it differently to reduce the impact on other tasks and improve system stability.

[PATCH 1/7] locking: add opt-in tracking of sleeping lock holders Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch introduces an opt-in tracking system to identify tasks that are holding sleeping locks, allowing the kernel to avoid stalls caused by memory allocations while holding shared locks. The system is based on each task counting the number of opted-in sleeping locks it holds, and provides a cheap way for other tasks to check if they should throttle or wait for a particular lock. Only the task itself can modify its count, eliminating the need for atomic operations. The tracking is disabled by default and only enabled when the first lock opts in.

[PATCH 2/7] locking/rwsem: track holders of opted-in rw_semaphores Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch adds a feature to track the holders of read-write semaphores (rw_semaphores) in Linux. It introduces a new flag, `tracked`, which is set when an rw_semaphore is opted-in for holder tracking. The count of holders is incremented when a task acquires the lock and decremented when it releases the lock. This feature helps to identify deadlocks by keeping track of which tasks hold locks. The patch also includes changes to handle cases where tasks may acquire or release locks in different threads, such as with CONFIG_DEBUG_LOCK_ALLOC enabled.

[PATCH 3/7] locking/mutex: track holders of opted-in mutexes Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch introduces a new feature to track the holders of mutexes, allowing for better debugging and analysis of lock contention issues. The feature is opt-in, requiring the CONFIG_TRACK_LOCK_HOLDERS configuration option to be enabled. A new function, mutex_track_holder(), is added to initialize a mutex with holder tracking enabled. The implementation covers both non-RT and RT kernel builds, including hooks in critical places such as __mutex_trylock_common() and __mutex_unlock_fast(). The tracked information is stored in the task's owner field, which requires alignment to 16 bytes due to bit 3 being used for this purpose.

[PATCH 4/7] locking/percpu-rwsem: track holders of opted-in percpu_rw_semaphores Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch adds a feature to track the holders of opted-in percpu_rw_semaphores, which are used in various parts of the Linux kernel such as filesystem freeze and async writes. The patch introduces a new function `percpu_rwsem_track_holder()` that allows opting in to holder tracking for a percpu_rw_semaphore. It also updates several functions, including `percpu_down_read_internal()`, `percpu_down_read_trylock()`, `percpu_rwsem_release()`, and `percpu_rwsem_acquire()`, to count the lock holders correctly. This ensures that tasks are never preemptible while holding a lock without being counted for it.

[PATCH 5/7] locking/selftests: add KUnit tests for lock holder tracking Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch adds 16 KUnit tests to verify the lock holder tracking functionality in the Linux kernel. The tests cover various scenarios, including locks that did not opt-in, mutex and rw_semaphore operations, downgrade_write(), cleanup.h guards, nested locks released out of order, percpu_rwsem_release()/percpu_rwsem_acquire() hand-off, and re-init clearing the opt-in. The tests ensure that task_nr_tracked_locks(current) always equals the number of opted-in locks a task holds, and also verify the behavior of lock holder tracking in different cases.

[PATCH 6/7] Documentation/locking: document sleeping lock holder tracking Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch adds documentation to the Linux kernel's locking subsystem, specifically about sleeping lock holder tracking. It explains why this feature exists, how locks opt-in to being tracked, what it costs in terms of performance, and where each lock type updates its count. The documentation also covers what is not covered by this feature.

[PATCH 7/7] kernfs, cgroup: track holders of the cgroupfs locks Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch optimizes the cgroupfs kernfs rwsems, cgroup_mutex and cgroup_threadgroup_rwsem by enabling lock holder tracking. This is done to prevent a stalled lock holder from stalling other unrelated operations in the cgroupfs control plane. The patch adds a new flag KERNFS_ROOT_TRACK_LOCK_HOLDERS that enables this feature for specific kernfs roots. It also modifies existing code to track lock holders for these rwsems and mutexes, which are held across memory allocation and other critical work. This change is intended to be used in conjunction with a policy that reads the count of lock holders.

This patch series introduces a mechanism to track sleeping lock holders in the Linux kernel. It adds a count to task_struct that indicates whether a task is holding a lock that other tasks may be waiting for. This information can be used by solutions for shared lock isolation scenarios, such as memory allocation and CPU limits. The goal is to prevent pressure in one workload from leaking into an unrelated workload through a shared lock, allowing the allocator to treat tasks differently based on their lock status.

[RFC PATCH 0/4] memcg_ext: memcg policy through cgroup-attached struct_ops 4 patches

Neutral LLM

The goal is to address the issue of tasks being stuck while holding kernel locks due to reclaiming and throttling. [PATCH 0/2] perf/x86/amd/uncore: Drop redundant counter slot searches 2 patches
Show 2 individual patches
  • [1/2] perf/x86/amd/uncore: Remove redundant event slot scan
  • [2/2] perf/x86/amd/uncore: Free counter slot by index
Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch optimizes the AMD uncore perf driver by removing redundant searches for counter slots in the amd_uncore_add() and amd_uncore_del() functions. The driver's serialization of event additions and deletions, combined with its inability to move installed events between slots, makes these searches unnecessary. By directly accessing the recorded slot index, the patch reduces the number of compare-exchanges required for deleting all events from a full PMU, resulting in improved performance.

Last comment 2026-09-25 [PATCH] rhashtable: specialize default comparison for constant parameters Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch optimizes the default comparison function in the Linux kernel's rhashtable module by utilizing compile-time constants when possible, reducing the need for dynamic memory access and improving performance. The patch achieves this by passing constant parameters from the caller to the comparison function, allowing it to use scalar comparisons instead of the generic memcmp() function. This change is expected to improve performance in various areas of the kernel, including scheduling, networking, and file systems.

Hello, Usama.

On Wed, Sep 23, 2026 at 02:34:18PM -0700, Usama Arif wrote:
>  		/*
> -		 * When dispatching directly from the BPF scheduler to a local
> -		 * DSQ, the task isn't associated with any DSQ but

dispatch_to_local_dsq() sets holding_cpu for ops.dispatch() inserts too,
not just direct dispatches. Can you name dispatch_to_local_dsq() instead,
here and in the description?

Clearing holding_cpu here is also how dispatch_to_local_dsq() learns that
it lost to a dequeue. The old comment on the other branch described that,
but for the wrong branch. Can you add a sentence about it here?

Can you also rewrap both comments at 80 columns? A few lines go past it.

Ongoing Submitted 2026-09-18 Last comment 2026-09-25 ⏱ No recent activity [PATCH v2] tick/nohz: Avoid unused timekeeping_max_deferment() calls Neutral LLM

This patch optimizes the tick/nohz code by avoiding unnecessary calls to timekeeping_max_deferment(). It checks the do_timer state first and only performs the seqcount-protected read when necessary, reducing accesses to timekeeper data. This change is particularly beneficial on x86-64, where it removes 18-20 dynamically executed instructions from the common non-owner path.

1 reviewer comment — 0 participants
0 participants
Usama Arif Neutral LLM
The patch is still awaiting review and feedback. The author has asked if there are any comments or reviews, but no substantive replies have been made yet.
Show original comment 
On Fri, 18 Sep 2026 06:34:08 -0700 Usama Arif <usama.arif@linux.dev> wrote:

> tick_nohz_next_event() limits a CPU's sleep interval to the maximum
> deferment supported by the current clocksource when that CPU owns the
> do_timer() duty. If the duty is unassigned, the limit also applies when
> the CPU's TS_FLAG_DO_TIMER_LAST flag is set.
> 
> After the early timer checks, the function currently reads the maximum
> deferment unconditionally. It then replaces the result with KTIME_MAX
> unless one of the two conditions above applies.
> 
> timekeeping_max_deferment() performs a seqcount-protected read of the
> shared timekeeper and follows its clocksource pointer. Check the do_timer
> state first and avoid this work when the result would be discarded. This
> leaves the resulting expiry unchanged and reduces accesses to timekeeper
> data that is modified regularly.
> 
> On x86-64 this removes 18-20 dynamically executed instructions, including
> the call, from the common non-owner path when the seqcount does not retry.
> 
> Signed-off-by: Usama Arif <usama.arif@linux.dev>
> ---
> v1 -> v2:
> - Remove the unnecessary comment and delta variable (Frederic Weisbecker).
> ---
>  kernel/time/tick-sched.c | 23 ++++++++++++-----------
>  1 file changed, 12 insertions(+), 11 deletions(-)

Hi,

Just wanted to check if there are any comments or reviews for this patch?

Thanks!
Usama

Last comment 2026-09-22 Re: [RESEND v7 08/29] mm: recognize PMD swap entries in the softleaf layer Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch modifies the softleaf layer to recognize PMD swap entries, addressing a warning that occurs when trying to access a non-present PMD as if it were a valid page. The change is made in response to zap_huge_pmd(), which is the only current path that reaches this condition. By recognizing PMD swap entries, the patch prevents unnecessary warnings and ensures correct behavior in this scenario.

Last comment 2026-09-21 Re: [PATCH v8 14/14] mm: thp: always enable mTHP support Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch removes a guard that prevents the start_stop_khugepaged function from running on systems without PMD leaves, allowing mTHP support to be always enabled even in such cases. This change is necessary because hugepage_enabled still returns true when the global always or madvise flag is set, which can lead to unnecessary resource allocation and increased min_free_kbytes. The patch proposes an alternative approach by testing whether an enabled order remains after masking PMD_ORDER when pgtable_has_pmd_leaves is false.

1 reviewer comment — 0 participants
0 participants
Luiz Capitulino ↳ replying to Usama Arif Inline Review Reviewed-by Needs Work LLM
Reviewer Luiz Capitulino noted that the patch's removal of a guard in start_stop_khugepaged() can lead to an idle khugepaged thread being started unnecessarily, raising min_free_kbytes, and suggested replacing hugepage_enabled()'s check for global configuration with a check for enabled orders after masking PMD_ORDER when pgtable_has_pmd_leaves() is false.
Show original comment 
You're right about the issue. Before this series, checking the global
configuration was sufficient to determine if THP was "enabled" as PMD
leaves would always be available (otherwise THP would be shut down).
With this series, we also need to check pgtable_has_pmd_leaves().

I prefer the simple fix below over masking orders in hugepage_enabled():
it's simple and adds the check only in the call site that requires it.

diff --git a/mm/khugepaged.c b/mm/khugepaged.c
index a3a9e4d93b46..175252a1d729 100644
--- a/mm/khugepaged.c
+++ b/mm/khugepaged.c
@@ -453,7 +453,7 @@ static bool hugepage_enabled(void)
  	 * Shmem pmd-sized hugepages are also determined by its pmd-size control,
  	 * except when the global shmem_huge is set to SHMEM_HUGE_DENY.
  	 */
-	if (hugepage_global_enabled())
+	if (hugepage_global_enabled() && pgtable_has_pmd_leaves())
  		return true;
  	if (anon_hpage_enabled())
  		return true;

On Fri, Sep 25, 2026 at 10:37:55AM +0100, Usama Arif wrote:
>
> That holds for the params given to rhashtable_init(), but not for the
> params passed to the fast-path helpers. The BPF resizable hashmap
> passes a constant template with no key_len and no obj_cmpfn

> copy it gives to rhashtable_init(). Without the "?: ht->p.key_len",
> its lookups would memcmp() zero bytes and return the first object in
